2026-08-01⭐ WDC(wiki-ingest) — UBS PT superseded, and the page now carries an explicit structural BEAR argument for the first time. 1 intra-quarter row, 1 Changelog entry.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — retired: UBS (Arcuri) Neutral, PT $145 (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22), the last UBS mark this page held. New: Neutral maintained, PT $560, rolled to a 2028 basis at an unchanged 10x multiple. The note itself states the move as "our PT increases from $375 to $560," which means an intervening $375 rung was never captured here — the $145→$375 step is undated on this page and is logged as a known gap in the PT history, not silently absorbed.** **Net-new substance beyond the PT: (1) UBS models FQ1 (Sept) at $4.4B/$4.66 against Street $4.0B/$3.73 — ~25% above on EPS — on EB +10% q/q, ASP +6% q/q and GM +500bps q/q to 57.5%; (2) the DOUBLE-ORDERING BEAR CASE, stated more aggressively than anything previously on this page: "customers will 2x order HDD capacity until demand starts to flatten, at which case revenue and EPS for HDD stocks could fall significantly," with UBS explicitly "pushing back aggressively" on the market's willingness to take a structural view of HDD while refusing one on memory — the direct inverse of the MS "HDDs are not memory" rebuttal set the page carries as its answer to the July de-rating. Both houses model a large beat; they disagree on whether the order book is real demand or duplicates; (3) a supply-side fact that undercuts the "no new capacity for 1-3 years" barrier — WDC, [[STX]] and Toshiba are "starting to buy heads externally from TDK," which is a route around the vertical-integration constraint that the structural bull case rests on. Flagged as a question for the 08-05 call rather than adopted as a thesis change.** Nothing else superseded — MS Overweight PT $650 (bull $920 / bear $322), BofA PO $732, GS Neutral $650, Barclays $620, Arete $610, TD Cowen $500 all stand. Also logged: Susquehanna (Hosseini) carries WDC **Neutral at $544.84** and published "Seagate: Reluctance to Acknowledge >15% Q/Q ASP Growth as Telling as 2027 Shortages" the same week (2026-08-01). Cross-filed [[STX]], [[MU]].

New: UBS NEUTRAL, PT €61. Note the €44 mark had already been flagged on 2026-07-27 as ~50% below the €87.71 Street median with spot €61.99 through it, which is why it sat in DIVERGES — the €61 refresh substantially closes that gap and the divergence should be re-scored at the next `/wiki-edge` run.** **Net-new substance: (1) FQ4 revenue guide expected ~€4.6bn implying +12% q/q against ~+5% normal seasonality, but segment margin 23.5% vs cons 24.1% — above on revenue, below on margin; (2) the FQ1-27 seasonality reiteration ("much less pronounced" than the usual −5/−6% q/q; cons −2.5%) named as the key call item; (3) 2026 AI revenue guide of €1.5bn expected to be RAISED to €1.5-2.0bn on capacity plus pricing, with 2027's €2.5bn left formally unchanged but carrying stated upside risk; (4) THE NEW BEAR LEG — 2027 AI MARKET-SHARE risk from internal capacity constraints plus [[ADI]] and [[TXN]] pushing into SiC stage 1/stage 2, i.e. the risk is share of a growing TAM rather than the TAM itself; (5) the China auto numbers quantified — domestic China auto sales (25-30% of auto revenue) −23% YTD y/y and NEV volumes −19% YTD y/y, plus a BMW warning (~5% of auto exposure); (6) the transmission mechanism to watch — restructuring in high-voltage auto (c.7% of auto revenue) on Chinese competition and pricing "could have spill-over effects on the high-voltage GIP segment, which we estimate represents >50% of GIP revs."** Nothing else superseded. Cross-filed [[ADI]], [[TXN]], [[ON]].
2026-07-31⭐ TSM(wiki-ingest) — the CoWoS outsourcing ratio is now quantified through 2028, and TSMC's gross-margin expansion is decomposed into price vs cost.** No TSMC rating or PT superseded (read-across plus third-party analysis). ⚠️ **The most reusable number added: the OUTSOURCED share of CoWoS goes 8% (2024) → 46% (2025) → 75% (2026E) → 85% (2027E) → 90% (2028E), with ASE's oS-outsourcing revenue $81mn → $697mn → $2,019mn → $3,809mn → $5,437mn** (JPM · Hariharan, ASE 2Q26, OW PT NT$650→NT$700, 2026-07-30). **The implication for how this page reads TSMC capacity commentary: with ~three-quarters of CoWoS already outsourced and heading to 90%, TSMC's own CoWoS capacity figures increasingly UNDERSTATE effective system capacity — CoWoS supply questions have to be answered at the TSMC+OSAT level, not TSMC alone.** ASE guided **LEAP revenue to at least DOUBLE in 2027 (implying $7bn+)** off a 2026 base already ahead of its $3.5bn guide; JPM models **~$4bn/$8bn/$12bn for 2026/27/28** and Jefferies-Fubon models **US$8.5bn in FY27, above company guidance**, with **FoCoS capacity including SPIL reaching 50k wpm by end-FY27** and ASE described as **"the most aggressive player in booking CoWoS tools"** (Shang, 2026-07-31). **Full-Process CoWoS is the 2027 driver — starting with AMD Venice this year, scaling in 2027, with possible extension to NVDA Vera or AWS Trainium depending on AMD execution; yield "progressing well."** ASE raised 2026 capex a SECOND time by $2bn to **US$10.5bn** across 20+ facility projects, and is lifting its structural ATM GM range from 24-30% to **above 30%** (Jefferies: mid-30s in FY27). ASE positioned as **"the primary partner for TSMC and the largest wafer-level packaging capacity provider outside of TSMC, much bigger than Amkor or Intel,"** with privileged access to leading-edge technologies **including COUPE**; panel-level packaging (310×310mm) ramps 1Q27, with CPO and EMIB contributing from 2027 and more meaningfully in 2028. **Separately, and materially for the margin debate: Chipstrat decomposes TSMC's recent gross-margin expansion as "gross margin rose 9.1 points, and manufacturing performance explained 3.2 of those points. The other 5.9 points? PRICING" — roughly two-thirds price, not cost, against management framing that points to utilisation and cost reduction. TSMC "historically played it safe, erring on the side of undercharging… That's changing"** (Lyons, 2026-07-31). Cross-filed [[ASML]], [[AMD]], [[NVDA]], [[AMZN]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[INTC]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).
2026-07-31⭐ TSLA(wiki-ingest) — three supplier/structural read-throughs, including the best external corroboration yet on Tesla's AI-silicon ramp.** No rating or PT superseded (2Q26 print marks from 07-22/23 stand). **① Panasonic reported a beat and raise with a ~50% operating-profit beat, with the desk explicitly naming "AI exposure shines well-beyond BBUs, TSLA BATTERIES & Blue Yonder beat — LONG PANASONIC"** (Amerian, 2026-07-30) — a positive volume/mix read on the energy and vehicle lines, independent of Tesla's own disclosure; Jefferies also flagged Panasonic and TEL results as strong. **② The most valuable item: from the test-handler supply chain, "Hon reiterated that TESLA'S AI CHIPS USE THE HIGHEST-SPEC HANDLERS, supported by THREE OSATS, with order momentum expected to remain strong from 2H26 through 2027"** (JPM · Huang/Tsai, Hon Precision 2Q26, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **Two inferences worth carrying: (a) "highest-spec handlers" places Tesla's AI silicon at the top of the thermal/test-difficulty distribution — Hon's customer roadmaps target ~10kW thermal capability in 2027-28 — consistent with a large, hot in-house accelerator rather than an incremental part; and (b) THREE OSATs supporting it implies deliberate supply diversification and non-trivial volume. This is one of very few external corroborations of AI5/Dojo-successor ramp timing, and it points to 2H26 through 2027.** Hon also expects ASICs to surpass GPUs within its AI order mix in 2027 — Tesla sits on the ASIC side of that shift. **③ China competition: Xiaomi's SkyNomad launched at a better-than-expected pre-sale price (BofA · Cheng; MS · Meng, "Positive Volume Outlook") — but the offset is a Xiaomi negative rather than a Tesla one, with JPM noting a LATER DELIVERY RAMP that puts Xiaomi's FY26 EV delivery target at risk** (Hariharan, 2026-07-30). Pricing pressure is real; the volume threat slips. ⚠️ **Logged as a reported claim, not a confirmed plan: "Elon Musk designed Tesla's China business to be EASILY SEPARATED from its U.S. business because of geopolitical tensions. It might also come in handy IF HE PROCEEDS WITH A SPACEX MERGER"** (WSJ, 2026-07-31) — the first public tie between the China-separability structure and [[SPCX]] merger mechanics, and live now that SpaceX is listed. Per this page's convention (no `## Intra-quarter` table), all of the above went into `## Debate / thesis` + this record. Cross-filed [[SPCX]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[TER]], [[TSM]].
2026-07-31⭐ TER(wiki-ingest) — four peer prints/notes that together give the clearest read yet on AI test intensity, plus one competitive warning.** No TER rating or PT superseded (all read-across). **The sizing anchor: BofA puts test intensity "sustaining at 7-8% of WFE, implying a $14bn+ ATE opportunity at $200bn WFE"** (Arya, 2026-07-30). **Cohu confirms the inflection from a peer P&L: Buy, PT $60 → $74; September revenue guided $170M vs Street $146M; Compute systems orders +150% y/y; CY26 HPC revenue raised to $105M from $90M; long-term HPC pipeline to $850M from $750M; utilisation at 80% and Industrial orders +87% y/y** (Jefferies · Garrigan/Curtis, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **But the Cohu funnel is early: only FOUR customers = $190M of annual opportunity are qualified today, with 15 more = $610-690M still in qualification and contributing nothing — the risk is conversion timing, not TAM.** **Chroma supplies the CPO and SLT detail: OW, PT NT$3,000 → NT$3,160; >50% share in CPO test Insertion 3 and 100% in Insertion 4.0 with shipments starting 3Q26; 2027 optical-comm revenue raised to NT$10bn from NT$7bn; transceiver light-source reliability test equipment orders >3x in 2026E; SLT modelled +50% in 2027 as 1H26's NVDA-GPU-only mix broadens to NVDA LPU, AMD GPU and Google CPU** (JPM · Tsai, 2026-07-30). **The single best test-intensity datapoint of the run: AMD GPU test time could be 2-3x NVDA's GPU at similar or slightly higher TDP** — a direct per-unit multiplier. ⚠️ **And a margin warning that applies to the whole test group, not just Chroma: input-cost inflation is now hitting test-equipment gross margins — Chroma's GM missed by 3ppt on material/component prices, and JPM cut its 2026-27E GM by 3-4ppt while raising revenue 10-11%.** **Hon Precision adds the handler/thermal layer: OW, PT NT$8,000 → NT$8,350; large-package ATC handlers (>120x150mm) carry a 20-25% price premium shipping from Oct 2026, with customer interest going from 1-2 to "almost every one" and a possible 20-30% of AI shipments in 1H27 rising to >50% in 2H27; customer thermal roadmaps target ~10kW in 2027-28; CPO insertion-4 co-test handlers ship on schedule in October as high-ASP option value; and within Hon's AI exposure management expects ASICs to SURPASS GPUs in 2027** (JPM · Huang/Tsai, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **Competitive warning worth carrying: Hon has completed only the FIRST stage of qualification on the Nvidia GPU SLT project, has NO confirmed GPU SLT order wins, and names COST STRUCTURE as its shortcoming vs its peer — the incumbent position in GPU SLT is being attacked but has not yet been taken.** Also: **China FT handler orders +>60% y/y and SLT +>80% y/y with Hon at 90-100% share of China high-end**, and a named list of OSAT capacity projects including **US facilities for both a Taiwan and a Korea OSAT** — onshoring is real and multi-vendor. 2026-07-31⭐ STX(wiki-ingest) — the memory shortage is now visible in hyperscaler capex and named to include hard drives, which is the cleanest demand read this page has had.** No rating or PT superseded. ⚠️ **The most useful item is a framing one: JPM ran its AAPL and STX post-earnings takeaways on the SAME analyst call (2026-07-31, 10am ET) — Apple's memory-cost problem and Seagate's memory-cycle upside are two sides of a single shortage. The mechanism: constrained and expensive flash pushes nearline capacity back toward disk, so the tightness that compresses Apple's gross margin supports HDD pricing and mix.** **The direct datapoint: [[AMZN]] named "inflated prices right now on some of the components like memory and HARD DRIVES AND SSDs" on its Q2 call, and attributed the whole $20bn increase in its FY26 capex guide (~$200bn → ~$220bn) to higher memory costs** (JPM · Cardoso, 2026-07-30). Customer-side corroboration: **Apple guided Sept-Q GM down with memory accounting for >100% of the sequential compression and said "for September, we expect to pay EVEN HIGHER memory costs"; UBS models DRAM and NAND ASPs +~30% q/q into December.** **Duration and mechanics from the memory complex: Samsung disclosed 60-70% LTA coverage with a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against increases open at 10-20%+ on 5-year rolling tenors; Kioxia is "on track toward 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28" and guides CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply"; TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The DRAM/NAND divergence in July is worth watching for this page specifically, since flat NAND contract pricing is a weaker push toward disk than the DRAM move implies.** BofA's sector read: hyperscalers are **"seemingly not bothered by rising prices of chips/memory,"** with Korean results implying tight supply and elevated pricing **"at least through 2027."** Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [[WDC]], [[MU]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SNDK]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

On Citadel's incentive: "Every 1% discount to the market price is $160MM in PnL/residual/alpha. At 3% it's $480MM, at 5% it's $800MM… Think of it like an overnight equity offering from an issuer. Only this was a fund of stocks that hit a MARGIN CALL from the prime brokers." MLP and Jane Street reportedly also bid** (Doug Garber / Pitch The PM, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The read-across is the float point already on this page: SPCX was described pre-print as a name where "the catalyst of the V3/starship launch has NOT STOPPED THE SHORTS FROM PILING INTO A LOW FLOAT STOCK." Also worth noting Aschenbrenner is not necessarily a retired participant — he retains a ~$5bn Anthropic stake and per one read "can probably raise $2B-$3B more."** **② Bernstein's 07-31 morning note lists "SPCX (feedback)" alongside AMZN, DDOG, PWR, AAPL and LHX — SpaceX is now in the standard daily client-feedback rotation, one day after the New Street piece, which means the bear framing is being actively litigated rather than ignored. 2026-07-31⭐ SNDK(wiki-ingest) — Kioxia's print gives the closest NAND read-across, and the message is that price is doing all the work while consumer demand contributes nothing.** No rating or PT superseded. **Kioxia missed modestly (revenue ¥1.76tn vs ¥1.84tn; OP ¥1.27tn vs ¥1.372tn consensus, OPM 75%; Sep-Q OP guided ¥1.9tn vs ¥1.95tn) against a bar already marked down — the stock went limit up pre-print, fell after hours, then recovered to ~+3%. But ASP was +70% Q/Q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only low-single-digit bit growth: the entire revenue move is price, and the volume restraint is deliberate. CY26 bit growth high-teens % "in line with Samsung guidance"; CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** ⚠️ **Structurally the most important disclosure: Kioxia is "on track toward 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28" — half of 2028 output pre-committed. Combined with Samsung's 60-70% LTA coverage (with a sub-5% q/q price-cut floor against increases open at 10-20%+, on 5-year rolling tenors), NAND/DRAM price formation now runs largely through contracts rather than spot, which changes what spot prints signal.** Capital return: **¥800bn buyback (30mn shares = 5.5% of shares out, 3 Aug → 30 Oct) plus a 3-for-1 split effective 1 Oct, funded from ¥187bn net cash and ¥827bn post-capex FCF.** ⚠️ **The key risk read for SNDK: agentic AI is "a primary growth catalyst" and server NAND is benefiting, but SMARTPHONE NAND DEMAND IS FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND A SLIGHT DECLINE on higher BOM costs — the cycle is carried entirely by datacenter.** Corroboration and a timing caveat from [[LRCX]]: **data centres increasingly demand SLC/TLC (not QLC) NAND for AI memory stacks — more expensive, more capacity, "a must" — but teed up for '28-'30, with NAND WFE actually DOWN 1H'26 vs 1H'25 in dollars.** ⚠️ **Positioning, logged so the move is not misread: SNDK was among the largest holdings in the force-sold Situational Awareness book and among the best performers on the unwind (+26% on 07-30) — the July drawdown was substantially forced selling, but the same fact confirms concentrated single-holder risk now transferred to a very different holder.** SNDK sits on BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list of names still 30-50% off highs. **MS SNDK 2Q callback with Joe Moore diarised 2026-08-06, 9am ET.** Cross-filed [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[LRCX]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
2026-07-31⭐ SMCI(wiki-ingest) — SMCI named as a direct beneficiary of NVDA's new GPU backstop programs, with a caveat that matters more here than for peers.** No rating or PT superseded. **The mechanism: NVDA is giving neoclouds a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity they cannot lease out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold; FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners. GS: "These backstop programs should broaden out compute availability to end customers by supporting continued neocloud growth. We view continued robust neocloud demand as positive for leading AI server vendors DELL and SMCI"** (Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **Given SMCI's heavier neocloud mix, the caveat lands harder here: the backstop moves demand risk from the neocloud to NVDA, sustaining ORDER FLOW regardless of whether the capacity is actually let. Near-term volume support, but neocloud order books become a weaker signal of end-demand, and the credit chain concentrates on a single counterparty.** Supply-side context in the same window: **Nscale is preparing a possible fall IPO at a floated ~$25bn (from $14.6bn in March) projecting 2027 revenue tripling to $30bn — with no announced tenant for its West Virginia flagship and "tens of billions" of financing still needed** (The Information, 2026-07-30); meanwhile **[[CRWV]] −25% and Sharon AI −42% over the past month.** Cross-filed [[DELL]], [[NBIS]], [[CRWV]], [[NVDA]].
2026-07-31⭐ SKHYNIX(wiki-ingest) — the LTA mechanics (asymmetric collars, not price locks) plus a positioning-driven +30% limit-up that should not be mistaken for news.** No rating or PT superseded. **Cycle mechanics: Samsung disclosed 60-70% LTA coverage with a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against increases open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING tenors; Kioxia is "on track toward 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28."** ⚠️ **These are downside collars with uncapped upside — high LTA coverage is bullish for realised ASP stability, not a cap on it, and it makes spot prints a weaker signal of realised pricing as coverage rises.** **July contract prints: DRAM +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31) — the divergence matters. Spot drivers into August: **restocking, Tier-2 OEMs resuming Sept/4Q launches despite cost, and China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at 17mn units in June vs a normal 25-30mn.** **China supply: "minimal impact… China-branded memory not commonly traded in the spot market."** ⚠️ **The positioning item, logged so the move is not misread as fundamental: KOSPI +18% overnight with SK HYNIX +30% LIMIT UP and Samsung +27%, as the forced liquidation of the Situational Awareness book to Citadel removed the marginal seller. JPM: Korea's l/s ratio has retraced to 2.8x (80th %-ile) from 6x, with net exposures ~60% off highs (vs KOSPI −40%) — but STILL +4.5z ABOVE THE 2018 AVERAGE. The crowding is reduced, not resolved.** Jefferies flags an unresolved structural risk: **Korean retail selling single-stock leveraged products while "aggressively purchasing INVERSE leveraged products" (one SK Hynix inverse 2x product −60% on the day), with "concerns around leverage in the system and retail margin calls [not having] all gone away after a 1 day bounce."** Context: **Korean ETF AUM ~W500tn across 1,000+ funds = 30%+ of KOSPI trading value**, with 2x leveraged ETFs an acknowledged volatility contributor, partly offset by new regulation and pension inflows. 2026-07-31⭐ SAMSUNG(wiki-ingest) — the post-2Q-call detail, and the single most reusable finding is that LTAs are asymmetric collars, not price locks.** ⚠️ **THESIS-DRIFT logged on the valuation basis: BofA raised 2026-28 EPS by 3-5% (on 2-3% higher memory volume/price assumptions and more favourable LTA terms) but CUT the target 2027-28E P/E from ~~10x~~ → **9x**, leaving the PO unchanged at W550,000** — near the top of the previous peak-cycle band of 7-10x (Woo, 2026-07-31). **The OP path is net-new and large: 2Q OP W89.5tn with DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND ~70% even after special bonuses; DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q (after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q), taking quarterly OP above W100tn (annual W400tn+) in 3Q/4Q26; then ASP flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs with annual OP still climbing to W500tn+ on volume and yield. Capex W78-100tn p.a. 2026-28; FCF W200tn+ (2026) → W300tn+ (2027-28).** ⚠️ **The trade being described deserves flagging: Samsung is deliberately giving price back "per AI server content increase" rather than defending ASP — a volume/share strategy, not price maximisation.** **THE LTA MECHANICS, which invert the common bear reading: 60-70% of Samsung memory sales go under LTA, but with a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against price increases broadly open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-year ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. High LTA coverage is therefore a downside collar with uncapped upside — bullish for realised ASP stability, not a cap on it.** Spot corroboration: **TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q, FLAT for NAND** (note the divergence); China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at **17mn units in June vs a normal 25-30mn**; and minimal spot impact from China local capacity because **"China-branded memory [is] not commonly traded in the spot market."** Capital return: buyback framed as **"when, not if"** on low treasury holdings, with 50% of FCF committed (Edward Kim, Head of Korean Equities); **no immediate ADR plan**; **foundry near breakeven in 2H ex-bonus on the 2nm ramp and new US customers.** Third-party mark logged: **KIS PT raised to KRW 650,000**, above BofA's W550,000 (@jukan05, 07-30). Stock **+21%** on the print; **KOSPI +18% overnight with SK Hynix +30% limit-up and Samsung +27%** as the Situational Awareness book cleared. Adjacent corroboration filed: **SEMCO 2Q OP more than doubled y/y on AI MLCC/substrates, annual OP W2tn in 2026 → W5tn+ in 2027, with prepayments funding capacity — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]], [[MU]], [[AAPL]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
2026-07-31⭐ RDDT(wiki-ingest) — the post-print revision wave. Every house cut its PT or its multiple; not one cut its estimates. That split is the whole story.** **PTs/multiples moved: JPM Anmuth Dec-26 $200 → Dec-27 $185 (Neutral); DB Black $250 → $200 — a 20% cut, the largest — on 25x → 18x FY27 EV/EBITDA while staying Buy; BofA Post PO $190 held but multiple 18x → 17x, with '27 revenue RAISED 7% to $4.6bn and EBITDA RAISED 8% to $2.2bn; MS Nowak PT $240 held with "model under review"; Redburn (Barker/Ball) reiterated SELL — the only outright sell.** The pattern is unambiguous: **numbers up, multiples down** — this was a de-rating on duration risk, not an earnings disappointment. **New theme row added to `Sinal vs gestão`: "Google dependency," which did not exist as a distinct debate before this print. GOOGL sized at ~40-50% of inbound traffic (MS) / "possibly 40%+" (BofA), with the mechanism identified as a ranking change down-weighting machine-translated content (international) plus weak CTR conversion in AI Overviews (US) — algorithmic and unilateral. Marked ✗ contesta.** Data-licensing row moved to **⚠ nuança**: JPM's disclosure that RDDT is trying to fold referral traffic into the licensing renewal ("many layers & dimensions… not necessarily all or nothing") means the non-committal tone from the call read as weakness rather than optionality. ⚠️ **Operationally important and easy to miss: RDDT will stop reporting logged-in vs logged-out DAUq going forward — Q2'26 is the last quarter the mix can be decomposed, precisely as it turns.** Best new decomposition: **US logged-in −100k q/q AND logged-out −200k q/q = −300k total US** (JPM); **US DAUs 53.2M vs 54.1M Street** (BofA). The most useful counter-argument on the page is DB's, and it is a conversion argument rather than a denial: **app users are ~4x more engaged and up to ~7x more monetizable than search-sourced web users**, and Reddit cut its app-conversion prompt from four visits/week to three during the quarter. Monetization milestones net-new: **ARPU +36% y/y to $6.18; >$1M revenue per employee; active advertisers +70% y/y; Max advertisers +60% q/q with Max revenue +150% q/q; ad revenue +64% against a 23pt tougher comp.** Stock **−23% on 07-31**. Cross-filed [[GOOG]], [[META]], [[SHOP]].
2026-07-31⭐ QCOM(wiki-ingest) — the Apple step-down is now quantified, and it is a cliff rather than a glide.** ⚠️ **THESIS-DRIFT: the page's Apple run-off framing is superseded in shape (not in end-point) — Qualcomm now guides an ACCELERATED step-down beginning in the Sep quarter with a 50% QOQ DECLINE in Apple product revenue in Q1-27 (Dec quarter)** (DB · Robert Sanders, "No longer the apple of my eye", Hold, PT $160, 2026-07-30). Prior on-page treatment assumed a smoother run-off into the April 2027 licensing expiry; a single-quarter halving front-loads the revenue loss materially. Second net-new item, and it is a margin-timing point rather than a demand point: **the soft Q4 profitability guide reflects higher input costs arriving BEFORE the benefit of Qualcomm's own chip price increases — a pass-through lag that compresses margin in the interim.** Segment detail added: **auto +61% y/y vs handsets −20% y/y within QCT** — diversification is working, but not fast enough to outrun the handset decline. Android offset logged with the appropriate scepticism: Qualcomm claims Chinese OEM weakness bottomed in the June quarter, which DB flags as likely to "arouse some scepticism"; **BofA independently corroborates "a modest China Android sell-in recovery into Q3"** (Arya, 2026-07-30), so the claim has one supporting house. DB keeps estimates **below management's own diversification targets.** Industry context filed: **rising input cost is pressuring QCOM and SWKS alike, smartphone units remain a 2H headwind, but content per device is rising** — the units-down/content-up split is the frame for the handset line. Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[ARM]], [[MU]].
2026-07-31⭐ NBIS(wiki-ingest) — three items that change how neocloud risk should be underwritten: a new listed comp coming, a supplier-written demand collar, and confirmation that July's drawdown was forced selling.** No rating or PT superseded. **① Nscale (UK, NVDA-backed) is preparing an IPO with GS and JPM, investor day in New York the week of Aug 3, potentially listing this fall. Bankers have floated ~$25bn vs a $14.6bn March valuation, and the company projects revenue TRIPLING to $30BN in 2027 — more than analysts expect [[CRWV]] to generate next year** (The Information · Valida Pau, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The specific risk to that number: the West Virginia complex acquired from American Intelligence & Power still needs "tens of billions" of financing AND HAS NO ANNOUNCED TENANT.** Nscale is also buying **Anyscale for $1.6bn**, explicitly mirroring CoreWeave's $1.7bn Weights & Biases deal before its own IPO; has raised **>$6bn** from 8090 Industries, Citadel and Jane Street; and added **Sheryl Sandberg and Nick Clegg** to the board. ⚠️ Tape context against the timing: **CRWV −25% over the past month; Sharon AI −42% over the past month** after being +60% since its February IPO; **Lambda delayed its own listing; Crusoe is talking to bankers.** **② The structural item, and the most important one: NVDA is writing GPU BACKSTOP PROGRAMS for neoclouds — a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity if the neocloud cannot lease it out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold. FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners** (GS · Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **This is a collar written by the chip supplier on its own customers' demand risk: it truncates neocloud downside, caps upside, and means reported neocloud capacity is no longer a clean read on independent end-demand.** GS views it as broadening compute availability and positive for [[DELL]]/[[SMCI]]. Also net-new and a genuinely specific customer reference: **Revolut pre-trained PRAGMA on H1000 GPUs in the Nebius cloud, reporting 65% more fraud cases caught, 2.3x better credit-default identification accuracy and 41% more relevant product recommendations.** **③ NBIS was among the largest holdings in the Situational Awareness book force-sold to Citadel, and was the best performer on the unwind at +26-27% on 07-30** ("NBIS +27%; BE +27%; SNDK +26%; CRWV +22%" — TMT Breakout). ⚠️ Reading both directions: July's drawdown was substantially a forced-selling artefact rather than a fundamental re-rating, **but** the same fact confirms NBIS carried concentrated single-holder risk, now transferred to a holder with very different behaviour. Fund detail: **~$20bn AUM, +400% in 2026, up to 4x levered, −67% in July; ~$16bn public book sold in one block; ~$5bn Anthropic private stake retained.** Cross-filed [[CRWV]], [[NVDA]], [[DELL]], [[SMCI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
2026-07-31⭐ MU(wiki-ingest) — the LTA mechanics were disclosed, and they invert how rising contract coverage should be read.** No rating or PT superseded. **The core finding: Samsung said 60-70% of its memory sales will be under LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against price increases broadly open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. Kioxia separately said it is "on track toward 50% LTA VOLUME COVERAGE FOR CY28 with key customers."** ⚠️ **These are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE, not price locks — so high LTA coverage is bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of the usual late-cycle reading. It also means spot prints are a progressively weaker signal of realised pricing as coverage rises.** **Pricing evidence net-new: Kioxia's Jun-Q ASP +70% q/q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only low-single-digit bit growth; Samsung DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND OPM ~70% in 2Q with DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q (after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q); TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q — but FLAT FOR NAND** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The DRAM/NAND divergence in the July contract print is the detail to carry; the two are not moving together.** ⚠️ **A ceiling on ASP upside also disclosed: Samsung is explicitly trading price for content — ASP guided flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs and "chipmakers' strategic control (e.g., decent price cut per AI server content increase)" — a share/volume strategy, not price maximisation, with annual OP still rising to W500tn+ on volume and yield.** Duration: **Samsung sees an "ongoing DRAM supply shortage due to AI, EVEN WITH new capacity expansion through 2027-2028"; Kioxia guides CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** **Spot drivers into August: restocking orders, Tier-2 OEMs resuming Sept/4Q launches despite high cost, and China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn.** On China supply: **"minimal impact… China-branded memory NOT commonly traded in the spot market."** ⚠️ **Best available confirmation of pricing power, because it shows up in the buyers' own numbers: [[AMZN]] raised FY26 capex ~$200bn → ~$220bn with the increment attributed EXPLICITLY to higher memory costs; [[META]] attributed its raise to component and memory pricing; [[AAPL]] said memory accounts for >100% of its Sept-Q GM compression, with UBS modelling DRAM/NAND ASPs +~30% q/q into December. BofA: hyperscalers are "seemingly NOT BOTHERED by rising prices of chips/memory," with Korean results implying tight supply and elevated pricing "at least through 2027."** Demand mix caveat: **agentic AI and server NAND are carrying the cycle — smartphone NAND demand is FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND a slight DECLINE on higher BOM costs.** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SNDK]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
2026-07-31⭐ MSFT(wiki-ingest) — the full Azure driver decomposition, the backlog-ex-OpenAI split, and one dissenting first-party view on Microsoft's own AI products.** No rating or PT superseded. **The single most important datapoint for the whole AI complex this week: backlog $678bn with +$51bn of the q/q increase driven by a BROADENING CUSTOMER BASE — "real AI demand from NON-FRONTIER MODEL CUSTOMERS" — and JPM's independent cut, commercial RPO +84% q/q but +25% EXCLUDING OPENAI.** That separates genuine enterprise breadth from OpenAI-specific commitments, which is the objection the July de-rating was built on. **Azure's five drivers, net-new: ① 31 DATA CENTERS added in the quarter (~1GW), on track to double capacity in two years; ② a multi-model strategy (OpenAI, Anthropic, Mistral, xAI, potentially DeepSeek) plus own models (MAI-Think, Code-1 Flash, Image-2.5) optimised for MAIA 200 at ~40% better performance/watt, with a 5X YTD increase in customers using MULTIPLE model providers; ③ Maia 200 at ~30% better performance per dollar, and Microsoft among the first clouds deploying HELIOS and VERA RUBIN, with Cobalt CPU VMs running Adobe, Arm, Elastic and OpenAI workloads; ④ POSTGRESQL REVENUE +55%, ACCELERATING FOR A THIRD CONSECUTIVE QUARTER (the stickiness argument: "migrating away becomes expensive and disruptive"); ⑤ FOUNDRY >100,000 CUSTOMERS with revenue MORE THAN DOUBLED y/y, FABRIC at 40,000 customers +60% y/y. Applications: COPILOT SEATS MORE THAN DOUBLED SEQUENTIALLY, 5M → 10M** (BofA · Liani hosted call, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The competitive comparisons in that list are management's own claims (Copilot 10% lower token usage; Code1 Flash matching GPT-5.6 quality at lower cost; Cyber1 at half a rival's price) and are flagged as such.** **Cross-hyperscaler basis warnings added, and there are now three: (a) GCP's headline acceleration (+21pp vs AWS +9pp, Azure +4pp) benefits from a smaller base and easier comp; (b) IaaS/PaaS mix differs across the three so growth rates are not strictly comparable (Barclays · Lenschow, OW PT $512, 2026-07-31); (c) GOOGL books TPU system sales GROSS while AWS books Bedrock NET (MS, see [[AMZN]]).** Any cloud league table this quarter needs all three. **Capex facts: C2Q26 +29% q/q / +69% y/y to $41bn including leases, ~two-thirds on SHORT-LIVED assets; ~$190bn CY26 guide reaffirmed ex an accounting change; C3Q26 guided $50bn+; FY27 capex to grow y/y. ⚠️ Effective FY27 Microsoft is EXTENDING the useful life of data centres and office buildings from 15 to 25 YEARS — which flatters reported depreciation, and sits awkwardly beside management's flexibility argument that short-lived-asset mix means "spend can be slowed if the demand environment changes."** **Stock +17%, biggest gain since 2008, ~$450bn added — the most in market history.** ⚠️ **Dissenting first-party view logged rather than filtered out: Matt Velloso (CPO Docker; ex-Microsoft, DeepMind, Meta) argues Microsoft's AI success is largely "a reflection of OpenAI's success," that GitHub Copilot is "em implosão" once its subsidy was removed and is squeezed by discounted GPT models and cheaper Chinese models, and that generic Copilot has no relevant assistant market share — directly contradicting management's 10%-lower-token claim on the same product. Bias disclosed: he is CPO at Docker. Logged as a live disagreement, not resolved.** Cross-filed [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[META]], [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[AMD]], [[NVDA]], [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).

2026-07-31⭐ META(wiki-ingest) — the capex debate now has two houses arguing opposite conclusions from the same print, and the contrast with [[AMZN]] is the most instructive fact of the week.** No rating or PT superseded. **① Redburn (Dom Ball via Falkiner, BUY, PT $1,000) is the only house on this page forecasting a capex PEAK: total capex peaking at $142BN IN 2027 then FALLING to $131BN IN 2028 — explicitly "15%/30% BELOW CONS." The mechanism is supply, not demand: an internal compute model projecting ~6GW OF OVER-CAPACITY BY 2030, hence "META HAS OVER-BUILT [and] a 'YEAR OF EFFICIENCY 2.0' WILL COME."** The strategic leg is the pivot from a trailing consumer LLM to a leading **SMB LLM / "business-in-a-box,"** with three new evidence points: **Zuckerberg on the call ("we'd like to build this into a business-in-a-box service that can help you start and run a whole business using Meta's platforms"); Alexandr Wang confirming on 26 July that "Meta will be launching a HARNESS" in 2H26 (a layer letting the model ACT, not just answer); and Muse Spark 1.1 leading on video/image-to-website generation.** Sized at **~0 → ~30% of Meta SMBs by 2030 at ~$78/month = ~$2bn-$80bn cumulative 2026-2030**, inside a **~$209bn opportunity at 45% IRR** on ~250m business distribution. On compute monetisation Redburn sides with Zuckerberg — **"the margin on selling intelligence is higher than raw compute"** — preferring a diversified AI-cloud offering to "a concentrated neo-cloud business selling to a handful of buyers." ⚠️ **The paired trade is a franchise call: BUY META vs SHOP (downgraded to Neutral) because the same SMB LLM "IN-SOURCES Shopify's most underappreciated engine, putting ~50% of its US volumes at risk and potentially HALVING group growth," on a ~90% ad-revenue correlation and SHOP at 52x PE** — see [[SHOP]]. **② The buy side read the identical print the opposite way, and not on magnitude but on narrative: "META CONFUSION… vision remains unclear. Capex was raised +$2bn at the midpoint, LESS than expected… TOO MANY OPTIONS ARE BEING KEPT OPEN? IT CREATES ROI QUESTIONS," with the print judged to have "FAILED TO… INSPIRE" next to the hyperscalers' ROI disclosures. Stock −8% on 13x core P/E** (BofA · Fenske/Liu via Villani, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The comparison worth carrying forward as a standing lesson: Meta raised capex LESS than expected and de-rated 8%, while AMZN raised capex $20bn MORE than expected and rallied 14%. The market is paying for ROI narrative, not spending restraint.** **③ Reconciled capex facts: C2Q26 capex +57% q/q / +83% y/y to $31bn; 1H26 $51bn (+66% y/y); FY26 NARROWED to $130-145bn from $125-145bn — a $5bn raise to the LOW END ONLY, implying 90%+ y/y growth. No reason given, but Meta had earlier attributed higher 2026 capex to component and memory pricing, so the raise may be cost rather than capacity.** Management declined a 2027 figure but framed plans around **"preserv[ing] flexibility for server decisions in 2028 and beyond"** — which, read carefully, is not inconsistent with Redburn's over-capacity thesis. Meta frames internal silicon as **"supply-chain leverage"** (JPM · Cardoso, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **Logged as an unverified CLAIM, not a datapoint: a report that Meta "has already committed almost $700 billion in future AI spending" with aggregate hyperscaler off-balance-sheet commitments at $3tn+, "double in one quarter" (@zerohedge, 2026-07-30) — needs confirmation against filings before use.** Cross-filed [[SHOP]], [[AMZN]], [[MSFT]], [[GOOG]], [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).

2026-07-31⭐ KIOXIA(wiki-ingest) — Jun-Q printed: a modest miss on a lowered bar, answered with ¥800bn of buyback and a 3-for-1 split.** No prior on-page number superseded (this is the new quarter). **The print: revenue ¥1.76tn vs ¥1.84tn expected; GM 78.1% (~20bp light); operating income ¥1.27tn vs ¥1.372tn consensus, OPM 75%. Sep-Q guided to revenue ¥2.39tn / OP ¥1.9tn vs ¥1.95tn consensus (¥3.16tn OP for the Apr-Sep half).** Jefferies argues the miss is optical against buy-side expectations that had already been marked down — the stock went **limit up pre-print**, dumped after hours, then recovered to ~+3% (O'Mara / Wong, 2026-07-31). **Net-new capital return: ¥800bn buyback, up to 30mn shares = 5.5% of shares out, 3 Aug → 30 Oct 2026; 3-for-1 stock split effective 1 Oct 2026 — funded from a net cash position of ¥187bn and ¥827bn of post-capex FCF.** **The pricing datapoint is the one to carry: ASP +70% q/q like-for-like on only low-single-digit bit growth — the whole revenue move is price, and the volume restraint is deliberate.** Cycle guidance net-new: **CY26 bit growth high-teens %, explicitly "in line with Samsung guidance"; CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** ⚠️ **The structurally important disclosure: "on track toward our 50% LTA volume coverage for CY28 with key customers."** Half of 2028 output pre-committed under LTAs — read alongside Samsung's 60-70% LTA coverage disclosed the same week, this is a NAND/DRAM market whose price formation now runs largely through contracts rather than spot, which changes what spot prints mean as a signal. Demand mix: agentic AI named a primary catalyst, server NAND benefiting, **smartphone NAND flat vs CY25 and PC NAND a slight decline on higher BOM costs** — i.e. the cycle is being carried entirely by datacenter. Two callbacks diarised: **JPM Kioxia debrief 08-03 8am ET; MS Kioxia/Sony/Renesas with Yoshikawa-san 08-02 9:30pm ET.** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

2026-07-31⭐ ASML(wiki-ingest) — ASML named Bernstein's Best Idea for 3Q26, and the pricing variant got its sharpest formulation yet. No rating or PT superseded** (Bernstein Outperform / €2,500 unchanged; the €2,300→€2,500 raise was already logged at the 07-15 print). The **timing** is the substance: the Best Idea designation lands **two days after Bernstein ran its own emergency client call on the China-DUV competition scare (07-28) and after an ~8% drawdown**, so the house is stamping highest conviction into the dip rather than into strength. **Net-new and the most reusable number added: litho intensity "rising steadily from 20% to close to 30% as DRAM migrates to more EUV layers, and stays elevated above 30% for advanced logic"** — the cleanest quantification of litho intensity on this page, and it recasts ASML as a share-gainer *within* WFE rather than WFE beta. Chipstrat (Austin Lyons, 07-31) makes it falsifiable — "the way the street has 2027 modeled, ASML's customers end up paying LESS per wafer of capacity than they do today. That has never happened" — which means flat pricing is upside and hikes are large upside.** Management's own language shift is now on the page as evidence: **April "not the way we do business" → July "more flexibility for pricing."** `Sinal vs gestão` **Pricing power row upgraded to ✓ confirms** with the two-mechanism decomposition folded in, plus the guardrail that ASML measures value on a **narrower yardstick than [[TSM]]** (throughput/overlay vs end-product value) and that over-taking invites both competition and regulators — not hypothetical two days after the China scare. Corroborating cross-read filed: **BofA's ASM International note argues deposition/ALD intensity is rising off the same drivers (GAA, HBM, 4F2 DRAM, advanced packaging)** — intensity is inflecting across the litho *and* deposition franchises at once (Scemama, 2026-07-31). NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, house estimate or existing NVDA number changed; purely additive.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH: the note's subject is the modular-datacenter vendor landscape (Vertiv/Flex/nVent/Quanta/Eaton/Schneider et al.), not NVDA.** Routed here because the note documents something the page did not carry at all — **NVDA's reference design has moved from the rack to the building.** What it adds: **DSX**, the reference design for the entire AI factory, dated — Omniverse digital-twin blueprint unveiled at **GTC Washington Oct-2025**, **formalized as the Vera Rubin DSX reference design in March 2026**, since expanded into the full DSX platform, covering compute/networking/storage/hardware-cluster design **plus the facilities side: power, cooling, controls, even civil, structural and architectural design.** The two named products, verbatim: **"Nvidia's DSX Max-Q maximizes token per watt within a fixed power budget, and DSX Flex facilitates the connection [of] the facility to grid services, dynamically adjusting power draw and orchestrating demand with hybrid onsite generation"** — DSX Flex pulls demand-response/BTM orchestration into the reference architecture itself. Deployment workflow via the Omniverse DSX Blueprint (digital twin → simulate layouts, power topologies, thermal behaviour, operational policies → optimize pre-construction → reuse across sites), with **CoreWeave already using DSX Air**. The adoption evidence is the point: Cadence, Dassault Systèmes, Eaton, Jacobs, Nscale, Phaidra, Procore, PTC, Schneider Electric, Siemens, Switch, Trane, Vertiv — **Vertiv's OneCore became the building block of the Vera Rubin DSX reference design as of March 2026** (12.5 MW pods), **Schneider works with NVIDIA on 30+ reference designs** (EcoStruxure Pod, up to 40 racks), **Siemens is among the first suppliers with both IEC and UL variants for Rubin DSX**, **Jacobs is one of only two engineering firms working with the DSX reference architecture (the other Bechtel)**, Hut 8's Beacon Point is designed around DSX, and Foxconn unveiled its Modular Data Center concept at GTC 2026. **Read: moat-widening beyond CUDA — it standardizes the building around NVDA's roadmap, so the switching cost of a competing accelerator now includes the facility. Flip side logged, not smoothed: a reference architecture reaching into civil/structural design also transfers more schedule risk onto NVDA's spec stability.** This also sharpens the PhotonCap caveat logged the same day from the Lumentum meeting ("it rides on one company's architecture choice") — that concentration risk is now documented on the power/cooling/EPC side as well as the optical side. 2026-07-30⭐ NVDAAurelion Research / PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum" (2026-07-29). 1 intra-quarter row (07-29, produto/mixed) + 1 Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, house estimate or existing NVDA number changed.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH: the subject company is Lumentum, and the source is a joint independent publication (management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap), not a bank; interview answers are the authors' paraphrase, not verbatim management statements.** **What it adds that this page did not have. (1) A supplier-side explanation of NVDA's CPO motive, from Lumentum management: CPO today is "opportunistic rather than forced. Nvidia deploys it because a faster, more power efficient laser frees up power that can be redirected to the GPUs, which buys system performance. Copper has not run out of headroom yet" — with copper hitting the wall "late 2027," when the first scale-up CPO deployments or MPO connectors arrive, driven by rack-to-rack links beyond ~3m. That CONFIRMS the earlier end of the CPO-timing range and sits against MS's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO *scale* and the unresolved Fubon post-Kyber rack decision already on this page; not reconciled. (2) An NVDA-sourced power number quoted by PhotonCap — "Nvidia's own material puts CPO at 3.5x lower power than pluggable transceivers" — with the mechanism stated as a fixed power envelope ("every watt saved on networking is a watt handed to the GPUs") and the conclusion "copper is not forcing this wave; the power budget is." ⚠️ PhotonCap's caveat retained verbatim because it is the systemic risk for the whole optical complex: "the weak spot is not the voluntariness but that it rides on one company's architecture choice." (3) Two Rubin/domain mechanics net-new here: Rubin "relies on bi-directional signaling to reach its bandwidth targets, and bi-directional signaling over copper degrades substantially at 448G per lane," with an unknown time-to-market for 448G copper; and NVDA "has begun moving from 72 GPU domains toward as many as 576 by 2027, and copper is already confined on an NV72 rack" — so once a domain spans racks, "linking must be optical regardless of copper's current efficiency." (4) Second-sourcing posture: "NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent," and "the same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B" — NVDA is not single-sourcing its external light source. Jensen's copper-for-scale-up / photonics-for-scale-out framing and NVDA's stated need for copper capacity both logged.** Cross-filed [LITE.md](LITE.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md), [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md).

Net: the thing you underwrite shifts from shortage PRICE to yield/mix DURABILITY. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** Scored against the tape — **Hynix opened -6% and recovered to -2.3% while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours** — the result is **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for the name, negative for the complex**; not the clean bounce, not the rout. **(3) The genuinely net-new analytical point, and it inverts the sign on the page's central negative: Sean names "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result" as one driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year (~24% next).** The same HBM4 slip that caused the revenue miss is **adding commodity DRAM bits to the industry** at a moment when commodity-DRAM returns are competitive with HBM (this page's own HBM-vs-EPS paradox, MS/Sean Kim 04-14). Folded into the HBM4 Sinal row as a three-legged read — share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, near-term bit/EPS cushion, and incremental supply feeding the cycle-durability bear — rather than logged as a separate positive. Also added: **the buyback was an expected catalyst that did not arrive** ("it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that," against a buyside pricing "the potential buyback as near-term catalyst"), now marked as a resolved miss in the Capex/capital-return Sinal row; and Sean's **CXMT-as-transfer framing** — a **US$492bn** debut close, 25th most valuable company globally, "quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix," with fundamental impact bounded to the outer years and **the 2030 expansion pace as the real question**. ⚠️ **Data-integrity flag raised, not adopted: the webcast's "CXMT currently at about 30,000 wafers capacity" cannot be reconciled with the ~290-350k wspm figures this page and [[MU]] carry from Wells Fargo (07-14) and BofA (07-12); treated as a transcription/sub-segment artifact.** ⚠️ **Date tension noted for the record: the webcast is filed 2026-07-28 but is internally pre-print ("earnings for Hynix tomorrow… Samsung on Thursday" = 07-30), while this page dates the Q2 print 2026-07-28.** The commentary is unambiguously pre-print and has been labelled as such throughout; the one-day filing discrepancy does not affect any figure. That is the bear case in one line — peak-margin cash committed to capacity into a forecast price peak. HBM inflation is real for NVDA (~+80%) but it buys ~10-15% cheaper than the merchant/ASIC field — a scale edge that partly offsets the ASIC-TCO bear this page carries from JPM/UBS.** On that BOM Fubon models the **Rubin ASP at US$78-80K on a 75-80% chip GM** (confirmed against the Beavington relay, not re-entered). **Net-new #3 — the fullest account on this page of what replaces Kyber, folded into the Product/Rubin Signal cell: Kyber was cancelled/delayed in MARCH/APRIL on mid-plane bandwidth issues; it carried 576 compute dies per rack (144 GPUs × 4 compute dies); checks say NVDA still targets 576 in the replacement, via either (a) four Oberon racks per system on traditional scale-out (144 compute dies each) or (b) a two-rack "Taycan" design at 144 GPUs × 2 compute dies per rack with NPO/CPO for the inter-rack scale-out and cable retained for intra-rack scale-up. Fubon explicitly declines to name winners and losers because neither is finalised — but says "nVidia is still aggressive in the NPO/CPO solution despite a lot of market noise."** Positioning kept in Fubon's own words on both sides: short-term the **NVDA supply chain "could potentially underperform due to Rubin's lower-than-expected volume in 2H26 and uncertain design change in the next generation rack design,"** with Fubon preferring the CPU/SPE/TPU chains — against **"token cost is still a more important factor in driving CSP's capex, and we remain positive about AI market demand."** **Two secondary sources added as separate rows. The buy-side point is that the returns math on the CURRENT generation clears a normal hurdle at every layer — the most direct available answer to the Crisafulli/Chanos/Burry "hyperscalers can't generate FCF" bear. No rating, PT, house or BBG estimate superseded.** **The material item is a disclosure that changes how an existing number on this page should be read rather than adding a new one.** Micron IR (Satish) stated that **the ~$100bn RPO across the 14 of 16 SCAs with a pricing framework is calculated as contract volume × the FLOOR price**, and volunteered the caution that **"the pricing is at the ceiling right now… the revenue that we will actually get on the SCAs, we expect will be well above the RPO levels. The RPO is not indicative of the revenue that we will get."** The page previously carried the $100bn as an "ASC606 accounting floor; actual expected materially higher" — that framing was directionally right and is now management-explicit, with the mechanism (floor-price arithmetic against ceiling-price realisation) named. **Second, the SCA architecture is now fully specified and it reconciles two figures the page carried side by side: up to 50%+ of total revenue under SCAs = ~40% priced-framework (band or fixed) + ~10% volume-only take-or-pay priced quarterly + ~50% market**, with the framework tranche "in the low 20%" today against the 25%-of-revenue/16-contracts disclosed at earnings — so management's ">50%" target and Barron's "~40% once fully executed" are the same plan on different tranches, not competing estimates. **All SCAs carry take-or-pay volume.** Ceiling confirmed ≈ the **June-calendar-quarter market price** (already inside the **86% Aug-quarter GM guide**, up from ~85%); floor GM again only *characterised* — "well above" the **fiscal-2018 low-60s%** peak, "we haven't quantified that level" — so **UBS's ~70-75%/$5-per-GB back-solve stays an inference and Bernstein's "meaningful but not infinite" bound stays untested.** **Third, the tightness horizon walked forward a third time** (2026 → 2027 → "beyond calendar 27 and even in 28"; "we actually do not see a line of sight to when the supply demand balance even comes back into balance") — logged as a walk-forward pattern, not a supersession, since the page already carried "no line of sight… through 2028." **Fourth, and the genuinely two-sided item: Satish conceded that customers are cutting the RATE of memory content growth versus prior plans, but attributed it to supply — "not… an optimization that truly reduces the amount of memory you need per unit of hardware."** Folded into the existing SemiAnalysis de-spec Risks bullet rather than logged separately, because the two accounts **agree on the fact and differ only on whether it reverses when supply arrives** — which is now the testable question on the content axis. Also new: **China exposure high-20s% → low-double-digit %** (Chinese-government restrictions on Chinese DC buyers + MU's own NAND-mobile abstention), **~3 nodes ahead of China / 1-gamma EUV >half of DRAM production / 1-delta next / ~65,000 patents**, and **100% of excess cash to be returned over time**. From the MS webcast (Joe Moore + Sean, 07-28): rank order **NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK**; the drawdown reframed as recurring rather than informational (**"you're not positive on Micron at 1200, you're positive at 800"**); the Q3 deceleration accepted outright ("the law of big numbers"); **Sean's three-prong debate resolving by elimination onto the cycle — 3Q y/y peak, 3Q DRAM +15-20%, 4Q needing +60-70% sequential to sustain the second derivative**; TrendForce **DRAM bits 25%→31% this year, ~24% next; NAND high-teens → 20-25% next year** (YMTC third line, 50,000 wafers); inventory up since 2Q **at module makers, not producers**; and corroboration that the **MU buyback waits until year-end on Chips Act restrictions**. Fubon (2026-06-05) had similarly held that "Intel EMIB-T is not yet MP-ready, so the V9 priority stays TSMC CoWoS-L" on a 60-70% EMIB yield vs CoWoS 95%+.** **New position (2026-07-28), verbatim: "Based on our checks, Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with four compute dies, suggesting that their capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027. Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and INTEL'S SUPPLY IS A MUST BY 2028." Plus: "although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement, we see Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain" — with EMIB capacity at 10-12k/month by end-2026 and a plan to double to 24-25k/month by end-2027, and Intel's CFO earmarking a large portion of the announced capex increase for advanced packaging such as EMIB-T.** **What changed, precisely, and it is not a yield capitulation: the 07-08 argument was that the TSMC fallback would absorb Google's demand; the 07-28 argument is that the demand is too large for TSMC to absorb, so Intel is required regardless of where the yield debate lands. The learning-curve caveat is explicitly retained — it is reclassified from "reason the ramp doesn't happen" to "risk to the ramp."** **Why it matters on this page: it removes the last major house arguing MediaTek's v9 has a clean TSMC CoWoS escape hatch — which is item (2) on MS's list of questions for the 7/31 call ("Intel EMIB-T production yield and whether there is a TSMC CoWoS backup plan"). The source of the backup-plan thesis has now answered it in the negative, on capacity rather than yield grounds. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with FOUR COMPUTE DIES, suggesting that their capacity consumption will MORE THAN DOUBLE in 2028 vs. 2027"** — a four-die package does not fit inside TSMC's book at that volume, regardless of what EMIB-T yields do. Fubon concedes the yield doubt it raised on 07-08 is unanswered ("still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement") but is now constructive on Intel *capacity*: **EMIB 10-12k/month by end-2026 doubling to 24-25k/month by end-2027**, CFO earmarking a great portion of new capex for advanced packaging; Ibiden's next call named as the tell. TSMC baseline added: **CoWoS 180kwpm end-2027 → 220kwpm end-2028**, with SoIC 4Q28 cut from 90k to 70k to prioritise CoWoS, and ASE FoCoS raised to 50kwpm. **Effect on the page's standing three-way v9 disagreement: it narrows to 2-vs-1 for EMIB-T** — Fubon crosses to join **UBS Asia (07-27)**, leaving **JPM · Gokul (07-08, "stays on CoWoS," Qualcomm/MediaTek-packaged)** as the holdout, while **UBS's own Arcuri (SemiBytes, 07-27) still calls EMIB-T "very low yields and immaturity."** **No view adopted; the divergence is narrower, not resolved.** ⚠️ **Second flag, deliberately left open: a basis conflict on the same 12-15mn number from the same house.** Jefferies · Sherman Chang relayed it on 07-17 (post-TSMC-2Q call with Fubon) as a **chip/die-basis** figure — "based on chips" — and Fubon now restates it as "12-15mn TPUs" against a V9 carrying **four compute dies per package**. Those cannot both be right at face value (12-15mn dies ≈ 3-4mn packages; 12-15mn packages ≈ 48-60mn dies). Both readings kept verbatim; for calibration UBS's 07-27 EMIB-T model has **2.3mn Google chipsets in 2028** at four XPUs per package, which is consistent with the package count being low-single-digit millions. **Third, and the sharpest cross-check against this page's own equity narrative: Fubon calls Google's 2028 target "very aggressive" EXPLICITLY "despite market concerns on Google capex and negative FCF"** — i.e. the packaging supply chain is being told to build for a plan the equity market punished with a -7.8% week on the first negative-FCF quarter. That is the supply-side/equity-side divergence stated by a source that has no equity axe on GOOGL (its top TPU pick is MediaTek). **On the MS note: the ROIC framework is the material addition, not the PT.** All three models standardise on **1GW of GB300 (410,256 chips, $39bn all-in capex/GW, ~$6bn/yr depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex, 21% tax)**, and **GOOGL is the only name MS puts on both sides** — bullish "hyperscalers AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT" *and* "model developers GOOGL and META." GCP leg (**~67% incremental EBIT margin, ~31% ROIC** at $8.50/hr) plus Gemini leg (**~75% margin, ~46% ROIC** on owned infra at 2,750 tok/s/GPU and $1.75/mn) is the first published statement that Alphabet captures both the landlord and the tenant spread on the same gigawatt — the quantified form of the vertical-integration argument Google's own Principal Architect made on 06-23. The rented-infra framework (**~25% NOPAT margin** at $7.75/hr) prices the ~$50/W SpaceX arrangement as a bridge cost. **Two caveats logged rather than buried:** MS states reported near-term incremental margins run **below** the band (prior-gen mix, traditional-cloud mix, unmodelled opex), so this does not contradict the 35.6% reported Cloud OI margin; and MS explicitly notes the frameworks are **GB300-based** — "while custom ASICs and prior GPU generation have lower costs… we believe chip pricing/hour and token throughput are also lower" — so **these are not TPU economics and should not be quoted as such**. BofA notes **14A upside is not contemplated in the guide**, and that TSMC capacity constraints (evidenced by the reported [[AVGO]]-Samsung foundry deal) make the Intel/Samsung positioning more valuable. **Numbers net-new from BofA that were not in yesterday's fold:** backlog was **expected to be seasonally DOWN** (low renewal year) and instead grew **~+$200m q/q** to $8.1bn; **cRPO $4.2bn ≈ 52% of backlog vs [[SNPS]]'s 45%** (better near-term visibility); **2Q China +24% q/q and +107% y/y**, with FY26 China guided to **grow ≥+19% y/y vs SNPS flat**; **Hexagon's ~28c FY26E dilution now fully offset by organic growth** (the $8.10 guide is back to the pre-Hexagon level, accretive from FY27); **SD&A +31% q/q, +32% y/y**. **Offsets logged honestly rather than buried:** **China is 15% of 2Q revenue vs SNPS's 11%** — the fastest-growing line is also the largest restriction exposure; **recurring revenue ~78% in 1H, below the ~80% guide and down from FY24's 83%** on IP/hardware strength; and **4Q pf-operating margin is implied down q/q** on SD&A/Hexagon/BETA CAE integration plus Intel 14A IP build investment (MS reads this as targeted investment framing FY27 upside, not demand weakness). No management guide superseded — FY26 $6.26-6.34bn / EPS $8.05-8.15 / OpM 43.75-44.75% and backlog $8.1bn all stand from the 07-27 print. 2026-07-28⭐ ASML(evening ingest)** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook + Twitter) — the China-DUV drawdown became the story, and the sell-side answered it with arithmetic. 5 intra-quarter rows + a Sinal/synthesis update. note it does not contradict the ~5/~20-unit arithmetic already on the page). **The best technical rebuttal came from outside the broker set and is worth keeping as a standing frame: @lithos_graphein defines the entry bar as a hard specification — "4.5nm MMO & >250 wph" — and offers the decisive natural experiment: Nikon is technically comparable and double-digit percent cheaper, and is shipping ZERO units this year**, because the binding constraint is **matched machine overlay**, not price or basic build capability (his published spec range for a 28nm immersion newcomer: floor **XT:1900i**, ceiling **NXT:1965Ci**). **Net read carried into the synthesis: nothing in this ingest changes the volume arithmetic already on the page (~5/~20 units vs the 131 immersion systems ASML shipped last year, qualification "many months or longer" away, domestic EUV still prototype); what changed is the price.** Also logged from the same BofA note (not ASML-specific, kept for the read-across): after a fireside with STM management, BofA's conviction on the data-centre story is rising — demand ahead of supply, capacity expanding at **Crolles 2 and Agrate**, and STM's **>$2bn 2028 data-centre target "increasingly appears conservative."** Cross-filed [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[SMIC]], [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md), [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md).
2026-07-28⭐ ASML**Backfill ingest of an unlogged PRIMARY company source: the ASML IR (Ted Shafer) small-group post-earnings call, 2026-07-20** (file imported 2026-07-21, never ingested — `grep -c "small_group"` returned 0). Ted Shafer, ASML Investor Relations, joined ASML 1991; **Capstone participated** (Felipe asked on price renegotiation, downturn history and the down-payment/escrow structure). **Additive — no PT, rating, estimate or guidance value superseded.** Logged as a *company* source, not a broker relay, which matters because most of what the page carries on capacity/allocation/pricing has been sell-side relay until now. **Net-new datapoints not previously anywhere on the page:** (1) the **EUV throughput ladder as a company statement — D 160 wph → E 230 (shipping now) → F 260 (phasing in next year, 2027)** — the mechanical basis for the "+30% units = +45% wafers/day" claim the page already carried from @SKundojjala (07-20) without the tool-by-tool numbers, plus the ASP/GM pairing (D ~€160M at low-50s GM → E ~€230M at high-50s); (2) **unit increments and the pad arithmetic** — the **+20 units in 2027 / +25 in 2028** framing came from an analyst on the call and was left uncontested rather than volunteered by ASML (flagged as such on the page), while Shafer's own contribution is the mechanism: **"each pad can support two or three units of output in a year"** on a cycle time he hedged as "I'm guessing it's about 20 weeks or so"; (3) **beyond-110 economics, the materially bullish item** — 110 is sized to "what we think the market requires" and fits the existing footprint, while going beyond it needs new footprint costing **"a few hundred million dollars or euros... 1 or 2% of our annual revenue"**, with "don't worry about, we'll add the shell if we have to" and shells assembled from supplier-built modules ("we're not building very much, we're assembling. So the cost is fairly low to add a lot more capacity") — i.e. the **company-level answer to JPM's 06-03 "110+ Low-NA without new buildings" thesis**, reframing the unit ceiling as a demand decision rather than a physical constraint; (4) **allocation mechanics** — a **"slot list that is like 100 tools long,"** allocation **"essentially first come, first served"** across ~5 EUV customers and explicitly *not* highest-payer-wins, with a PO required **12-18 months before the on-dock date plus a down payment that is "just a percentage of the purchase order," not negotiated tool by tool** and held as cash rather than escrow ("you'll see it show up in our free cash flow"); (5) **the order-book nuance that cuts against the 07-17 London Roadshow mark** — *"if you look at our commitments, **we're not completely booked for 2027**, even though we also have commitments for 2028... there's some holes in the list as customers towards the end start to push things out a little bit"* — folded into the Signal-vs-management **Capacity** row as a nuance against the stronger "2027 nearly fully booked" framing the page carried from 07-17 (both retained; they are reconcilable — 2028 commitments alongside 2027 gaps from customers spacing deliveries — but "sold out" should not be read as literal); (6) **"We don't report bookings anymore,"** an explicit confirmation of the disclosure gap that sits under the didier (07-15) backlog-value-at-Q4-report (Jan '27) catalyst already on the page; (7) **High-NA capacity is fungible to Low-NA** ("the units required for high NA are not at the full capacity that we have planned for. So maybe you could use some of that capacity for the low NAs as well"), plus the reliability KPI made explicit — availability **"80% plus" today, ~90% the threshold, hoped by end of 2026** — and adoption detail: **Intel in production at 18A** (not 14A), plugged in **"just like for like"** with yield "at least as good as low NA"; **SK Hynix "very vocal about wanting to bring it on as fast as possible,"** Samsung following, TSMC waiting for A10 on Low-NA-fleet ROI; revenue-neutral to ASML (2 Low-NA ≈ 1 High-NA at 2x price) but **Low-NA margins better today**, so early High-NA delay is GM-accretive; watch **customer announcements at the October technical conference** ("don't trust us... the customers will tell you"); (8) **Match Act / export controls, and IR is more relaxed than the tape** — ASML already cannot ship EUV nor the most advanced immersion to China, ~20% of revenue from China this year; the Match Act targets **all immersion tools**, but where the end-Q1 framing allowed a possible 2026 impact, **"in Q2 nothing passed and I think the risk for this year is zero"** (both chambers must pass, president must sign, then the Dutch and Japanese have limited time to decide how to comply; reports put odds of passage at **"25% or less"**; redirected tools would "find other homes" given immersion demand) — logged as a counterweight to the 07-27 Jefferies and The Information China-litho bear rows; (9) **pricing/fairness mechanics that bound the pricing-power upside case** — **one list price across memory, logic, China and Korea**, ASP differences from volume discounts only, **"the only way to be fair given our dominant market position,"** the same price to Samsung's logic and memory divisions, and **"No risk allowances made"** for cyclical memory customers (no price cuts when memory margins were poor: "we don't like the model of chasing our customers' margins"); order-flexibility precedent — **Intel placed many POs for a new Ohio fab and ASML "just redirect them to other business from Intel"**; (10) **"those Terafab numbers are in our analysis for going up to 110 in 2028"** — Terafab is already inside the capacity plan, not upside to it; (11) corroboration of the unit path from the company — **44 Low-NA shipped 2025 → "mid-60 units, let's say 65" in 2026 → +30% 2027 → +30% 2028, "going 30 plus 30 takes us to 110 units per year, roughly."** Also captured in the row: IBM upgrades worth **~another 10% of factory output** (65 factory tools ≈ 6-7 tool-equivalents of install-base upgrades, e.g. D 160→190 wph at ~€30M), used this year to serve customers whose cleanrooms were not ready; the **+6% GM frame is a two-year-old "opportunity space," not guidance, and "things are looking a little better"** with new numbers due at the **CMD in June 2027**; immersion carries ASML's **best margins of all product lines**; IBM GM ~60% the past two quarters; visibility built bottoms-up ~2yr+ from customers' own customer data merged into a top-down GW/hyperscaler view for 4-5yr; **15 consecutive years of growth**. **Edits: 1 Current-state capacity passage (beyond-110 economics folded into the JPM 110+ discussion), the Signal-vs-management Capacity row (throughput ladder + cheap-ceiling + the not-fully-booked-2027 nuance), 1 Intra-quarter Full-log row (07-20, supply/bull, marked PRIMARY), the Q2'26 Síntese (07-20 addendum), and 1 Sources entry.** Nothing on the page was overwritten.

2026-07-28⭐ AAPL**/wiki-ingest (Outlook). 1 intra-quarter row. No rating/PT superseded** (BofA Buy $380 reiterated). **Apple formally launched “Apple Upgrade” in the US** — a hardware-**leasing** platform spanning iPhone, Watch, Mac and iPad, sold through the online store, the Apple Store app and retail, and **financed via Klarna**; 12/24-month terms on iPhone and Watch, 24/36-month on Mac and iPad, from **$17.99/mo (iPhone)**. **The mechanism is the point, and it is a genuine change to how Apple captures value from the installed base rather than a new financing SKU.** BofA's articulation: under purchase/installment, Apple monetises the device fully up front but **the customer owns the residual**, which Apple only recaptures if the customer elects to trade in; under a lease, the customer pays less per month and **returns the device**, so **Apple monetises the residual itself**. The net incremental is therefore **lease payments + returned-device value + a faster replacement cycle**, with secondary benefits from **lower affordability friction, premium mix, and greater direct control of the customer relationship** — the last being strategically consistent with Apple's long-running push to disintermediate carriers. BofA is careful that the underlying mechanics (trade-ins, monthly financing, the iPhone Upgrade Program) are not new; **what is new is packaging them into one platform across all four major hardware categories.** Logged as additive product/strategy evidence for the installed-base-monetisation leg of the thesis; **no estimate or guidance impact quantified by the note, and none inferred here.**

2026-07-27⭐ SMIC**/run-inbox — The Information (Staff), "China Starts Mass-Producing Homegrown DUV Chipmaking Tools, An Advance for Local Chip Industry" (2026-07-27). Additive; no rating, PT, estimate, guidance or management figure superseded.** Ingested and labelled throughout as **single-outlet, anonymously-sourced ("two people familiar") news reporting — not a company disclosure, not a broker note, no rating/PT/estimate**; SMIC did not respond to a comment request. **Net-new to this page:** (1) **SMIC is a named 2026 delivery recipient** (with Hua Hong Semiconductor and CXMT) of the **first domestically mass-produced immersion DUV lithography machines**, built by a **Shanghai-based state-backed company the article deliberately leaves unnamed**, which pulled immersion-DUV development teams from other Chinese firms **including the state-backed equipment startup Shanghai Yuliangsheng Technology**; prototypes went to some chipmakers last year and encouraging test results triggered production this year. (2) **The sizing that keeps it honest — ~5 machines in 2026 and ~20 in 2027 against the 131 immersion DUV systems [[ASML]] shipped last year** — so this is a strategic-direction datapoint, not a 2026-27 capacity datapoint. (3) Four gates, all quoted rather than paraphrased: the tools **"still trail ASML's products in performance and build quality"**; mass-production insertion **"could take many months or longer"** as chipmakers test accuracy, reliability and tool-compatibility; **mostly domestic content but some key parts still come from Japan**, with **local-supplier delays holding back this year's output**; and China's **domestic EUV remains prototype-stage and "likely years away"** from working chips — i.e. **the EUV ceiling on SMIC's roadmap is unchanged**. (4) **First application is mature-node capacity, including power-management components essential to AI servers/data centers** — it maps to the 12-inch legacy base and the AI-power-management pull management flagged in Q1'26, not to N+3. (5) **Independent corroboration of the multiple-patterning workaround** this page documents from the SemiAnalysis/STEEL teardown: "SMIC has used that approach to produce advanced chips for smartphones and AI systems despite lacking access to EUV." (6) **A new, sharper policy exposure: the Match Act would curb SERVICING of tools at some Chinese fabs in addition to widening immersion-DUV sales restrictions** — the page previously carried only the sales/service *ban* framing from the AlphaSense ASML primer (2026-06-22); servicing degrades the **existing** ASML fleet's uptime and yield, and ~5-20 domestic units/yr cannot backfill it. (7) **A demand item from SMIC's own management that this page did not carry: CEO Zhao Haijun said on the May earnings call that overseas customers were looking to make chips in China because capacity was tight elsewhere** — a non-domestic demand leg against an ~85-89% domestic revenue base (⚠️ second-hand — The Information's characterization of the call, not a transcript line captured here). (8) Structural backdrop: lithography a Chinese national priority since **2002**; the **2022 US controls pushed Beijing from state-led research toward commercial execution with Huawei coordinating industry resources**. **Edits: 1 bullet under Advanced-node / Huawei supply line, 1 bullet under Current state (the Zhao Haijun demand point), 1 Catalysts bullet (domestic-DUV qualification watch), 1 Risks bullet (tool-servicing risk as distinct from tool-purchase risk), 1 Intra-quarter Full-log row (07-27, supply/mixed), a new "Tooling / supply" row in the Signal-vs-management table plus an append to the Demand/2H-risk row, an append to the Q1'26 "AI / memory-driven tightness" cell of the Management-commentary table, a Quarter-synthesis append, and 1 Sources link.** Deliberately NOT added: any claim that the domestic tool relieves SMIC's advanced-node constraint (the article says the opposite on EUV and gives a mature-node first use case), and no restatement of the Match Act's SMIC naming, which the page already carries from the AlphaSense primer. Cross-read [[ASML]] (folded there this run as a bear/structural-risk datapoint sized against the 131-unit base rate).
